5b

Operating leverage

2 answers
C-7552P-0158
C-75

How material is the fixed-cost operating leverage: how much does a given change in revenue move operating profit, and how wide a range of outcomes does that create?

D5219th of 35

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement. At group level operating leverage is moderate, because most costs move with volume and metal is passed through; in Transmission it is high, because plants and vessels are fixed and utilisation is lumpy. The practical consequence is that group EBITDA is reasonably predictable for given volumes, but a single loading event at Halden (the replacement Mass Impregnated order) moves the Transmission margin by well over 100 basis points, and the FY2028 range of plausible group EBITDA outcomes is about €900m to €1,150m around a central €1,020m to €1,050m.

  • The company's own sensitivities (2024 registration document): a 1 percentage point change in group revenue moves EBITDA by about €11m and earnings per share by €0.18 (about 2 percent); a 50 basis point change in EBIT margin moves EBIT by about €34m and earnings per share by €0.58 (about 7 percent). €11m on about €61m of revenue is an incremental margin of about 18 percent, roughly 1.5 times the average margin: moderate leverage.
  • Transmission: H1 2026 EBITDA rose 21 percent on flat sales, but that was contract vintage and selectivity, not volume leverage; the downside leverage is under-absorption, which is what the Great Sea Interconnector halt threatened (Bernstein modelled the Transmission margin falling to 12.2 percent on under-absorption and weaker bargaining power). Halden's mass-impregnated line, three vessels and Charleston are fixed costs; a half-year of an idle line plausibly costs €25m to €40m of EBITDA. Electra adds fixed cost but removes variable charter cost; it raises leverage both ways.
  • Grid: medium leverage; capacity additions (about 40 percent by 2028) bring depreciation and staff ahead of volume, and margins rise as frameworks fill them.
  • Connect: low leverage; costs are largely variable (metal, compounds, freight), so volume swings move profit roughly proportionately; margin moves come from mix and unindexed input costs rather than from absorption.
  • Range of outcomes for FY2028 group EBITDA: downside about €900m to €950m (Bernstein's stacked downside of about €900m; Deutsche Bank's earlier €933m): the replacement order slips, Connect Europe stays weak, Republic Wire's synergies come late; central €1,020m to €1,050m (consensus €1,052m); upside €1,100m to €1,150m (the company's range, which includes further acquisitions, or the Great Sea Interconnector resuming). Roughly -12 to +10 percent around the centre.
P-01

After assessing the fixed-cost operating leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, against the last four to six years?

C+5811th of 35

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. Fixed-cost operating leverage at Nexans is moderate at group level and will shape the forecasts less through volume than through three dated fixed-cost events, and the net effect is half the margin expansion of the last five years, arriving more mechanically. The three events are: (1) Transmission's fixed asset base (Halden, Charleston, three vessels, Charleroi from mid-2027) moving from under-absorbed, legacy-priced work in 2025 and early 2026 to fully loaded work at 2023 to 2026 prices, which takes Transmission EBITDA from about €204m to about €298m between FY2025 and FY2028 on revenue only 9 percent higher; (2) Grid's 40 percent European capacity step and 39 percent accessories step, which bring staff, start-up costs and depreciation ahead of volume in 2026 and 2027 and pay back in 2028 as six- and seven-year frameworks fill them; and (3) a rising depreciation base (€176m in FY2024, €254m in FY2025 or about €244m excluding acquired intangibles, €285m in FY2026 and €330m by FY2030 on that basis), which is itself the largest new fixed cost and keeps EBIT leverage below EBITDA leverage until 2027. The adjusted EBITDA margin on standard sales rises from 11.9 percent (FY2025) to 13.4 percent (FY2028) and then plateaus at about 13.5 to 13.6 percent to FY2030; the EBIT margin rises from 7.9 percent to 9.2 percent and then 9.5 percent. Against FY2020 to FY2025, when the margin roughly doubled from 6.1 percent to 11.9 percent through selectivity, portfolio rotation and a 50 percent jump in Transmission volume in 2024, the next four years add about 170 basis points, and the leverage is asymmetric: its downside sits in Transmission after mid-2028, when the mass-impregnated line at Halden has no visible order.

The size of the leverage, measured three ways.

  • The company's own sensitivity (2024 registration document): one percentage point of group revenue moves EBITDA by about €11m, an incremental margin of about 18 percent against an average of 11.9 percent, a leverage ratio of about 1.5 times.
  • My base case: FY2025 to FY2028 incremental EBITDA margin on standard sales of 19.5 percent (€285m on €1,465m of added standard sales), which is consistent with the company's sensitivity. The leverage is therefore not exceptional in aggregate; what is exceptional is where it sits.
  • By segment, FY2025 to FY2028 (FY2025 segment figures are my reconstruction from the company's mix percentages; FY2026 onwards are my forecasts):
Segment Standard sales FY2025 to FY2028 EBITDA FY2025 to FY2028 Margin FY2025 to FY2028 Incremental margin What drives it
Transmission 1,650 to 1,798 (+9%) 204 to 298 (+46%) 12.4% to 16.6% about 64% contract vintage, Electra replacing chartered vessels, a loaded Halden; not volume
Grid 1,342 to 1,718 (+28%) 218 to 282 (+29%) 16.2% to 16.4% about 17% volume on new capacity, accessories mix, offset by start-up and staffing ahead of volume
Connect 2,317 to 3,277 (+41%) 291 to 413 (+42%) 12.6% to 12.6% about 13% mostly Republic Wire scope at its own margin; organic leverage low
Other Activities about 790 to 770 15 to 20 shrinking metallurgy by design

Transmission: where the leverage is, and how it shapes the lines.

  • The evidence that this is absorption and vintage rather than volume: H1 2026 Transmission EBITDA rose 21 percent (to €106.5m, a 13.7 percent margin) on organic sales down 0.1 percent; management attributes it to dilutive legacy frameworks rolling off through late 2026 and refusal of concessions. The peer precedent is NKT, whose Transmission margin went from 15.3 percent to 20.2 percent in one year on newer contracts while still carrying about 200 basis points of ramp cost (DNB Carnegie, 1 September 2026).
  • My path: 14.3 percent in FY2026 (H2 about 14.8 percent with Electra's first full half), 15.6 percent in FY2027, 16.6 percent in FY2028, then 16.5 and 16.3 percent in FY2029 and FY2030. This is about 40 basis points below management's 17 percent for 2028, because I assume Halden's mass-impregnated line is loaded only to mid-2028 (the replacement order's stated horizon) and then runs on partial and repair work. The previous chief executive guided to margin gains of 100 to 150 basis points a year from 2026 (February 2025 call); my path is at the middle of that.
  • The quantified downside of an idle line: Bernstein put the Great Sea Interconnector halt at about €100m of lost annual sales at Halden (19 June 2026). Mass-impregnated cable carries a high share of fixed conversion cost (a dedicated line, its staff and depreciation), so I estimate the contribution lost at 40 to 50 percent of that revenue: about €20m to €25m of EBITDA for each half-year the line runs empty, or €10m to €15m net of the repair and inspection work Nexans has used as filler (the Svenska kraftnät frame agreement, August 2026). That is 1.5 to 2.5 percent of FY2027 group EBITDA and about €0.25 to €0.35 of EPS per half-year. The base case assumes the replacement order is signed by the end of the first quarter of 2027; each quarter of slip beyond that costs about €5m to €8m.
  • The asymmetry: in the last high-voltage surplus, executed margins fell from 17.9 to 5.7 percent at Prysmian (2017 to 2018) and from 13 to 7 percent at NKT's Solutions business (2017 to 2018) as lines went empty. Nexans's backlog of about 4.5 years of sales protects 2026 to 2028; the exposure is H2 2028 onwards, which is why I flatten the Transmission margin after FY2028 instead of extrapolating it.

Grid: leverage that runs negative first, then positive.

  • European capacity rises about 40 percent between 2025 and 2028, accessories capacity 39 percent (€62m, 2026 to 2028), and a medium-voltage plant in Morocco ramps through 2027 and 2028. These costs arrive before the volume: H1 2026 Grid margin was 15.4 percent against about 16.1 percent a year earlier, with Q2 growth "capped by capacity".
  • My path holds the Grid margin at 15.8 percent in FY2026 and 16.0 percent in FY2027 (start-up costs plus a share of the 2027 energy-cost step), rising to 16.4 to 16.5 percent from FY2028 as frameworks fill the lines and accessories (about twice the margin of base cable) rise in the mix. Revenue grows about 9 percent in FY2026 and FY2027 and 8 percent in FY2028, above the company's mid-single-digit guideline, because the Q3 2026 organic growth of 14.8 percent and the doubling of Enedis volumes show that demand exceeds the capacity being added.

Connect: little fixed leverage, so margin is a mix-and-input-cost story.

  • Costs are largely variable (metal, compounds, freight), so volume swings move profit roughly proportionally; the margin fell from 13.6 to 11.8 percent between H1 2025 and H1 2026 on Nordic weakness and dilutive Southern European and Latin American mix, not on absorption.
  • The one fixed-cost question is Republic Wire's 30 percent capacity expansion, fully online in December 2026, which must be filled in a market where Nexans holds about 4 percent against an integrated duopoly. I hold Republic Wire at about €66m of EBITDA in FY2027 (the acquisition-case figure) rather than assuming the new capacity fills at entry margins.
  • The European plant footprint (including the Nordic plants) is the only large latent lever in either direction; P-03 covers it.

Depreciation: the fixed cost that matters most for EPS.

  • D&A rose from 3.2 percent of standard sales (FY2024) to 4.2 percent (FY2025) and about 4.3 percent in FY2026 (H1 2026 at €153.4m). The drivers are dated and known: Halden's doubling (2024), Electra (€380m, in service June 2026), Republic Wire's plant, Charleroi (mid-2027), Lens recycling (2027), Grid and accessories capacity and Morocco (2027 to 2028).
  • Because D&A rises about €41m in FY2026 and €15m a year thereafter, EBIT grows more slowly than EBITDA in FY2026 (+10 percent against +11 percent on my figures) and faster from FY2027 (+17 and +15 percent). This is why FY2026 earnings per share are flat while EBITDA rises.

Against the last four to six years. FY2020 to FY2025 margin expansion was "heroic" (management-driven): SHIFT's selectivity (about €300m of structural EBITDA from 2018 to 2024), the exit from low-margin businesses, and Transmission volume up 50 percent organically in 2024 on the doubled Halden. FY2026 to FY2028 expansion is mostly mechanical and dated (contract vintage, Electra, capacity filling, synergies), which makes the path more predictable but smaller. After FY2028, my base case has no further leverage source of size: Transmission plateaus, Grid normalises to mid-single-digit growth, and Connect's margin creeps up only with Republic Wire's synergies.

Skew and cone. FY2028 EBITDA plausibly lies between about €920m and €1,100m around my €1,013m. The downside (€920m to €950m) combines the replacement order slipping two quarters, Halden unloaded from mid-2028, Connect Europe weaker and Grid start-up costs lasting longer. The upside (€1,060m to €1,100m) combines Grid sustaining double-digit growth into 2028, a Transmission margin at 17 percent in 2028, and earlier Republic Wire synergies. The skew is mildly negative in Transmission and mildly positive in Grid; at group level it is roughly symmetric before 2028 and negative after.

5c

Financial leverage

C-7662P-0254
C-76How material is the financial leverage or gearing: how much does it amplify earnings per share and risk, and how comfortable is it?C-6215th of 35full answer under 4c Financial profile
P-02

After assessing the financial leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share and the balance sheet over the next 18 months to four years?

C-5416th of 35

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. Financial leverage is low, fixed-rate and falling, and it will shape earnings per share in three distinct phases rather than by amplifying them. In FY2026 it is the main reason EBITDA growth of 11 percent produces flat earnings per share: net financial items roughly double, from €53m to about €100m (acquisition debt for Republic Wire, the bridge loan, negative carry on €1.4bn of cash, and a foreign-exchange hedging loss in H1 2026). From FY2027 to FY2030 it becomes a modest tailwind as net financial items fall to about €55m (worth about €0.70 of cumulative EPS if no acquisition is made). And from FY2028 the most important variable below EBITDA is not interest rates but how management spends a balance sheet that, without further acquisitions, falls to about 0.5 times EBITDA by end-2028 and near zero by end-2030: on my arithmetic a €500m buyback at today's price adds about 7 percent to FY2028 EPS, while a €500m acquisition at 9 to 10 times EBITDA adds about 4 percent, and management has said it prefers acquisitions. The base-case EPS path is therefore the floor of the capital-deployment outcome, and the company's €1,150m 2028 ambition requires about €1bn to €1.3bn of acquisitions that the balance sheet can carry within its own 1.0 times ceiling by about 2028 and comfortably within the BB+ rating.

The debt stack (all fixed-rate; company net debt excludes leases).

Instrument Amount Coupon Maturity Note
Sustainability-linked bond €400m 5.50% April 2028 +50 basis points in the final year if 2026 greenhouse-gas targets are missed (about €2m)
Bond €575m 4.125% 2029
Bond €350m 4.25% 2030
Bond (Republic Wire refinancing) €500m 4.25% September 2031 issued September 2026, replaced the bridge loan
Leases €178m about 3.5% €3m of lease interest in H1 2026

Cash was €1,456m and net debt €1,038m at 30 June 2026 (1.4 times on the company's ratio; FactSet's 1.51 times includes leases); average cost of debt 4.1 percent in H1 2026; covenant 3.2 times; rating BB+ stable.

How net debt moves (base case, no unannounced acquisitions).

  • End-2026 about €790m (about 1.0 times): H2 FCF of about €75m, about €190m of Autoelectric proceeds (sale closed 3 July 2026 at €207m enterprise value), about €15m of anti-dilution buybacks.
  • End-2027 about €690m (0.8 times), end-2028 about €545m (0.5 times, after the Republic Wire earn-out of up to €43m), end-2029 about €320m, end-2030 about €75m: cumulative FCF of about €1.5bn over FY2027 to FY2030 against about €630m of dividends and €100m of buybacks.
  • The €400m sustainability-linked bond (5.5 percent) is repaid from cash in April 2028 in my base case, which removes the most expensive tranche; the 2029 bond is refinanced at about 4.5 percent.

Net financial items and their EPS effect. About -€100m in FY2026 (of which roughly €25m to €30m is the non-recurring H1 hedging loss and bridge-loan costs), -€85m in FY2027, -€75m in FY2028, -€65m in FY2029 and -€55m in FY2030. Each €10m is about €0.16 of EPS. Interest-rate sensitivity is negligible: the debt is fixed, and 100 basis points on the cash balance moves net financial items by about €14m in the company's favour.

The capital-allocation arithmetic that will decide the EPS path after FY2027.

  • Capacity: at the company's 1.0 times ceiling, about €0.5bn of unused debt capacity at end-2028 and about €1.0bn at end-2030; at 2.0 times, which Bernstein calculated is consistent with BB+ (24 February 2026), about €1.5bn more.
  • A €500m buyback at €114 retires about 4.4m shares (about 10 percent of the diluted count). After about €14m of lost interest after tax, FY2028 EPS rises from €9.56 to about €10.25 (+7 percent).
  • A €500m acquisition at 9.5 times EBITDA adds about €53m of EBITDA; after about €8m of D&A, €20m of interest and tax, FY2028 EPS rises to about €9.94 (+4 percent) on the adjusted basis (before purchase-price amortisation), and dilutes return on capital.
  • Management's stance: acquisitions are "a central capital-allocation priority" with "a rich worldwide pipeline"; buybacks are formulaic and anti-dilutive (176,005 shares from July 2026); Bernstein notes buybacks could become the alternative if acquisitions do not materialise. I therefore treat the base case as the floor: the most likely outcome is one or two acquisitions totalling €0.5bn to €1.3bn by 2028, adding 4 to 10 percent to FY2028 EPS before amortisation and keeping leverage at 1.0 to 1.5 times.

The balance sheet beyond debt.

  • Goodwill rose by a provisional €474m with Republic Wire; acquired intangibles (provisionally valued, still to be allocated) will be amortised at about €15m to €18m a year, which adjusted EPS excludes and reported EPS does not.
  • Equity rises from about €2.0bn (end-2025) to about €2.6bn by end-2028 on retained earnings (consensus book value per share is €49.53, €55.00 and €62.30 for FY2026 to FY2028), so gearing falls on both numerator and denominator.
  • Return on capital employed (pre-tax, company basis) fell to 15.0 percent in H1 2026 from 21.3 percent in FY2025 because of the acquisition and capacity spending. On my EBIT path and an estimated €3.6bn to €3.8bn of capital employed, it recovers to about 18 to 19 percent by FY2028: short of the above-20 percent target without EBITDA upside, and further from it if the next acquisition is priced like Republic Wire.
  • The Republic Wire earn-out: up to €43m payable in 2028 on performance through 2027. The H1 2026 report is quoted as carrying a discounted value of €86m, which is inconsistent with a €43m cap; I model €40m of cash in FY2028 and flag the discrepancy as a question for the next set.

What would change this. A second Republic Wire-sized deal at 10 times would hold leverage at 1.3 to 1.5 times through 2028 and add about €25m to €30m of interest; a missed sustainability target costs about €2m in 2028; an antitrust outcome above the €75m provision would be a cash item, not an interest item.

Cost-out scope NTM

C-7740P-0344
C-77

How much of each major part of the cost base is cuttable or reducible?

D4019th of 35

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement. Little of the cost base is cuttable in a way that would protect profit in a downturn, because metal is passed through rather than saved, the high-voltage fixed costs are strategic, and SHIFT already took out the easy overhead. The realistic reducible pool is in Connect Europe's plant footprint, purchasing and digitally enabled overhead, worth perhaps €40m to €80m over several years.

  • Raw materials (about 70 percent of cable production cost): not cuttable; passed through. Savings come from recycling (Lens; 25 percent recycled copper content by 2028) and purchasing scale, and in the United States from Republic Wire's purchasing synergies (about half of €23m).
  • Conversion costs (labour, energy, depreciation): energy secured 18 months ahead, so it lags rather than falls; labour is partly flexible in Connect and Grid; Transmission's skilled workforce and assets cannot be cut without losing capability.
  • High-voltage fixed costs (Halden, Charleston, Charleroi, three vessels): strategic; the 2018 to 2020 precedent is plant closure (Hanover land high voltage) only after prolonged under-utilisation.
  • Selling, general and administrative costs: SHIFT removed two management layers in 2018 and 16,000 low-value accounts; SHIFT AI is presented as worth about €40m of EBITDA by 2028; the new Chief Operating Officer is tasked with footprint mutualisation, purchasing efficiency and supply-chain performance.
  • Connect Europe footprint: the most plausible large lever if construction stays weak (Nordic plants); none has been announced.
P-03

After assessing the scope for cost elimination and efficiency in greater detail, how much is likely to reach the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, and when?

D4423rd of 35

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. Nexans has identified about €60m to €75m of gross efficiency for 2027 to 2029, but only about €30m to €40m of it is likely to reach adjusted EBITDA as a net gain by FY2029, and very little before FY2028, because the programmes are mostly purchasing and digital efficiencies that run against 3 to 4 percent annual wage inflation and an energy and polymer cost step in 2027. The identifiable sources are Republic Wire's synergies (€23.6m run-rate within three years, half purchasing and half commercial; I include about €6m in FY2027, €15m in FY2028 and €22m in FY2029), SHIFT AI (presented at about €40m of EBITDA by 2028; I treat about half as net of inflation) and the new Chief Operating Officer's purchasing and footprint work (from 1 November 2026, no target yet; earliest observable effect H2 2027). There is no large cut in the base case: SHIFT already took out the easy overhead (two management layers and 939 positions in 2019; 16,000 low-value accounts), metal is passed through rather than saved, and the high-voltage fixed costs are strategic. The one material latent lever is consolidation of Connect's European plant footprint, including the Nordic plants, worth perhaps €20m to €40m a year after two to three years and about one to one-and-a-half times that in restructuring cash; I put about a 30 percent probability on an announcement by the FY2027 results if Nordic construction stays weak, and it would show first as reorganisation cost (excluded from adjusted EBITDA but in cash) and only later as margin.

The programmes, what they are worth, and when they reach the line items.

The offsets that absorb most of it.

  • Wage inflation: personnel costs of roughly €0.8bn to €0.9bn a year on the continuing perimeter (my estimate from about 12,800 employees) rising 3 to 4 percent a year is about €25m to €35m a year that must be recovered through price or efficiency.
  • Energy: Nexans buys energy at least 18 months ahead, so the September 2026 jump in European electricity (+97 percent year on year) and gas (+133 percent) reaches 2027 costs; with polymers and compounds after the Strait of Hormuz disruption, I carry a net headwind of about €20m in FY2027, mostly in Connect and Grid.
  • Start-up costs of new capacity in Grid, Morocco and Charleroi in 2027.

Why there is no large cut in the base case. About 70 percent of production cost is metal, passed through rather than saved; Transmission's assets and skilled workforce cannot be cut without losing capability; and European labour law makes plant closures slow and expensive (about 5.8 percent of the cable workforce is temporary, which gives some volume flexibility). The precedents are the 2019 restructuring (939 positions, two management layers, Hanover land high-voltage plant closed) and €87m of reorganisation provisions in 2020; both followed a profit warning. Management has no published plan to merge Finnish or Swedish plants.

What reaches the cash flow. Reorganisation costs were €34.3m in H1 2026 (€18.7m a year earlier) and are recurring in practice; I carry about €60m of reorganisation and integration cash in FY2026 (including part of the €23m of Republic Wire integration costs, which are capitalised) and about €30m, €25m, €20m and €20m in FY2027 to FY2030. Bernstein has argued (January 2026) that their recurrence makes their exclusion from adjusted earnings questionable; I agree, and my FCF deducts them even though my adjusted EBITDA excludes them.

Against the last four to six years. From 2018 to 2024 SHIFT delivered about €300m of structural EBITDA, roughly €40m to €50m a year, the main source of the margin doubling. The next four years have perhaps a fifth of that pace of net self-help; the margin expansion now has to come from Transmission conversion and Grid capacity (P-01) rather than from cost.

5e

Reinforcing feedback loops

2 answers
C-4758P-0552
C-47To what extent do gravitational or mechanical forces, rather than heroic company dynamism, improve the company's competitive advantage and its financial outputs against peers with each period that passes?C+588th of 35full answer under 4a Business quality
P-05

After assessing in greater detail whether reinforcing or balancing feedback loops are at work in the business model, such that dynamics at play today create additional scope for further positive/negative dynamics in the near/mid-term (reinforcing feedback loops), or the opposite (balancing loops), how are they likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

C-5214th of 35

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

The Investment Analyst's heading After assessing in greater detail whether reinforcing or balancing feedback loops are at work in the business model, how are they likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

Judgement. Two reinforcing loops drive Nexans's profit and loss account today, while two balancing loops already act on its cash flow and order book; the gap in timing between them is the most useful single shape for the forecasts. Through FY2028 the reinforcing loops dominate earnings: the Grid capacity loop (added capacity shortens lead times, which wins longer frameworks, which fills the capacity and justifies more of it) and the scarcity loop's late phase, in which prices already set during the shortage convert into executed Transmission margin as legacy contracts finish. The balancing loops act first on cash and order intake: the down-payment loop (a flat backlog means new down-payments roughly equal those consumed, so working capital no longer releases cash, and turns into an outflow if awards stall) and the customer cost loop (high prices push transmission operators to re-phase projects, coordinate procurement and, in Germany, return to overhead lines, which reaches award timing and down-payment terms before price). From 2028 to 2030 the balancing loops reach revenue and margin: the scarcity loop closes as incumbents' and entrants' capacity lands (parity about 2030), and the selectivity loop (value over volume cedes share, which shortens the backlog, which exposes Halden's utilisation after mid-2028) caps Transmission volume. The forecast shape that follows is: EBITDA growth of 13 and 11 percent in FY2027 and FY2028 decelerating to 5 and 4 percent in FY2029 and FY2030; free cash flow below 40 percent of EBITDA until FY2029 rather than recovering to the 45 percent target in 2028; and a Transmission margin that plateaus at about 16.5 percent instead of converging on peers' 20 percent.

The loops, their state, and their effect on the line items.

Loop Type for Nexans now Evidence (dated) Effect in my base case What would reverse or strengthen it

Scarcity loop (late phase: price to executed margin) Reinforcing for executed margin through 2028; balancing for new-award prices from about 2028 525 kV price per kilometre doubled 2019-20 to 2024-26 (DNB Carnegie); Nexans Transmission margin +195 bp on flat sales (H1 2026); entrants qualified or winning (Hellenic Cables July 2026; LS Cable at TenneT; Sumitomo's Nigg plant opened 19 October 2026); Morgan Stanley parity about 2030, 15% oversupply if European offshore wind runs at 13 GW a year Transmission margin 12.4% (FY2025) to 16.6% (FY2028), then flat at 16.3 to 16.5% Faster grid investment (the EU Grids Package, €530bn to €540bn 2025-31) would extend scarcity; an entrant bidding low on a European 525 kV award would bring the plateau forward
Selectivity loop Balancing (for volume) About 3 to 4 points of European subsea share lost (a Prysmian strategy director, August 2026); backlog €7.8bn (about 4.5 years of sales) against NKT €13.0bn+ and Prysmian €18bn to €20bn; no visible mass-impregnated work beyond mid-2028 Transmission revenue -4%, +6%, +7%, +2%, +2% (FY2026 to FY2030); a mild under-absorption allowance from H2 2028 A signed replacement mass-impregnated order with a further award behind it; a Great Sea Interconnector restart (Meridiam's 66% stake, seabed surveys)
Down-payment loop Was reinforcing (2024-25), now neutral, can turn balancing Cash conversion 83.1% (H1 2025) to 42.7% (H1 2026); working-capital inflow €202.5m to €121.1m; backlog flat for nine months; NKT's free cash flow -€249m in H1 2026 as down-payments slowed before capacity (the clearest peer case) No down-payment release in FY2027 to FY2030; working capital an outflow of about €30m to €35m a year as Grid and Connect grow; FCF 28 to 41% of EBITDA Stall in awards (no replacement order, no 2027 awards) would add about €100m to €150m of outflow over FY2027-28 (10 to 15% down-payments on about €1bn of missing awards); a large award (Great Sea Interconnector restart, a second TenneT call-off) would release cash
Customer cost loop Balancing, early TenneT re-phasing 7.7 GW by up to a year; Elia postponing on "unprecedented price increases"; Energinet pausing connections; five operators coordinating subsea procurement (April 2026); Germany's draft law prioritising overhead lines for unawarded direct-current corridors (April 2026; undergrounding up to 4.5 times the cost) Award timing slips rather than prices falling: part of my lower Transmission volume in FY2027-28 against consensus (+6 to 7% against about +10% a year) Affordability politics (the US Ratepayer Protection Act, September 2026) would strengthen it; regulated returns (UK RIIO-T3, Cyprus's 3.7% premium) weaken it
US protection loop Reinforcing for Republic Wire now; balancing later 50% Section 232 tariff; US copper wire producer prices +27% year on year; but Canadian rod blocked; incumbents adding capacity; Atkore margin halved from 30 to 15% when supply normalised Republic Wire EBITDA €66m (FY2027) rising only with synergies to about €88m (FY2029); no price-led growth Extension of tariffs to finished cable (Prysmian's US unit asked for it, August 2026) strengthens it; a US price war or construction slowdown reverses it
Copper loop (my addition) Reinforcing for reported revenue and profit now; balancing when copper falls Copper at a record (about $13,000 per tonne, September 2026); Core exposure gain €75.2m in H1 2026; copper supplier payables under negotiated arrangements €449m (end-2025) Current-metal revenue +26% in FY2026 with no adjusted-profit effect; working capital higher; reported EPS flattered in FY2026 A copper fall reverses reported gains (a non-cash loss) and releases working capital (helps cash): reported and adjusted EPS will diverge in the opposite direction
Track-record loop Neutral for the forecasts, important for the multiple Cable stocks re-rate on confirmation of guidance rather than surprise (NKT, Prysmian 2026); Nexans's raise in July 2026 was acquisition-led None on the numbers; it argues that FY2027 guidance (24 February 2027) will be set below consensus by habit An organic raise or a 2030 target set above consensus at a capital markets update

The interplay that matters most. The selectivity and down-payment loops are linked: because Nexans has not committed speculative subsea capacity, it has fewer new awards to collect down-payments on, so its working capital normalises earlier than NKT's, but it also carries less utilisation risk when parity arrives. That is the trade the forecasts embody: lower cash conversion in FY2026 to FY2028 than the market is used to from Nexans, in exchange for a smaller margin cliff after 2029 than NKT faces (Transmission is about 20 to 25 percent of Nexans's EBITDA against about 80 percent at NKT).

Against the last four to six years. From 2021 to 2025 every loop that matters ran in Nexans's favour at once: scarcity raised prices, down-payments funded working capital (cash conversion 47 to 68 percent), selectivity raised margins without visible cost, and copper scarcity favoured the integrated maker. From 2026 the loops diverge: two still reinforce the profit and loss account, two now restrain cash and orders, and by 2029 the balance tips to restraint. This is the main reason I expect earnings growth to be strong but short-dated (FY2027 and FY2028) and free cash flow to lag earnings throughout.

Group judgement

Guidance

C5813th of 35

Layer 1 · Guidance

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Nexans guides conservatively, specifically and from contracted building blocks, and it tends to beat or meet; its weakness is that its upgrades are infrequent and, in 2026, acquisition-driven, and that it has never raised the 2028 ambition the way peers have raised theirs. FY2026 guidance sits just below consensus and is achievable in its upper half; FY2027 guidance in February will probably be set below consensus by habit, which the market has historically read as cautious rather than negative; the 2028 ambition is an organic-plus-acquisitions figure and should not be modelled as organic. The informal tone is more confident than the formal ranges. The October omission of profit and cash data, and an important order announced before it was signed, mean the next set should weight the February 2027 results heavily and management statements lightly.

D4521st of 35

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement. Guidance is slightly below consensus for FY2026 and there is none yet for FY2027; the 2028 ambition sits above consensus only because it includes acquisitions consensus does not model. The pattern reflects deliberate conservatism (excluding the Great Sea Interconnector and future acquisitions; assuming only what is contracted) set against a consensus that has extrapolated Q3's Grid and Connect strength.

  • FY2026 EBITDA: guidance €770m to €840m (midpoint €805m) against a FactSet median of €817m (mean €807m; Jefferies' compiled consensus €820m). Consensus sits about 1.5 percent above the midpoint; Bernstein warned before the update that consensus was about 1 percent above the midpoint and needs about 140 basis points of second-half Transmission margin improvement. The midpoint implies H2 2026 EBITDA of about €417m, up about 17 percent on H2 2025's €356m (restated), of which Republic Wire contributes about 7 to 8 points; consensus implies about €429m, up about 20 percent.

How conservative is current guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five years, adjusted for the difference in input change forces versus the past?

C-74

To what extent have sell-side estimates been cut deeply and fully across all analysts, and has company guidance recently been rebased lower, or more aggressively kitchen-sinked or sandbagged?

C+488th of 35

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement. Neither. FY2026 estimates were cut materially over twelve months, but for identifiable reasons (the Great Sea Interconnector removal and the perimeter change) and not across the out-years; guidance was set conservatively in February 2026 and then raised, which is mild sandbagging, not a kitchen-sink. The setup is therefore not a "cuts are done" rebound; it is a stock whose estimates are roughly at guidance, whose revision momentum has just stalled, and whose ratings are bullish.

  • Estimate revisions (FactSet mean earnings per share): FY2026 -13.9 percent over twelve months, -1.6 percent over six, -3.3 percent over three, -2.8 percent over one; FY2027 -3.2, +5.1, -0.9 and -2.6 percent; FY2028 +2.4, +12.0, +1.7 and +0.7 percent. On the dashboard's FY2027 series, earnings per share -3 percent over one month and -1 percent over three, EBITDA flat and +3 percent, sales -1 percent and flat, target prices -2 percent and flat; the revision-momentum score fell from 7 at the end of September to 1.
  • Breadth and ratings: the buy share rose 22 points over three months to 69 percent; ten of the twelve visible broker sheets are Buy or Overweight (Equita Hold at €147; Bernstein Market-Perform at €150 outside the sheet); no Sell ratings; mean target €168, 48 percent above the price.
  • Guidance: the February 2026 range excluded the Great Sea Interconnector and was "surprisingly light"; it was raised in July (mostly by acquisition) and confirmed in October.
  • What it means: the asymmetric rebound that follows a full capitulation (deep, broad cuts and a kitchen-sink guide) is not available; the risk instead is a further modest trim to FY2026 and FY2027 if the second-half Transmission margin or the replacement order disappoints, against a ratings base that has little room to become more bullish.

Case simplicity

Risks

C5813th of 35

Layer 3 · Risks

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The forecasts for the next 18 months are more predictable than the share price's behaviour and the company's reputation suggest, and less predictable than their EBITDA line alone would imply. The two earlier sets are congruent on every operating judgement and jar only in bases, and the one apparent contradiction in the file (the model's 13 percent earnings shortfall against the second set) is a FactSet half-year artefact that my input changes remove; once corrected, the model reproduces the second set's EBITDA, EPS and cash within the definitional residuals the second set itself predicted. About two-thirds of the expected change to 2028, and about four-fifths of the change over 18 months, rests on strengths Nexans has demonstrated (contract-vintage conversion, delivered capacity and vessels, Grid demand, European integration), and the strengths still to be shown (US low voltage, a Connect margin without a construction recovery, Halden after mid-2028, cash conversion above 45 percent on a full basis) sit mostly in 2028 and in the lines where consensus, not the second set, is exposed. The drivers are concentrated by name (three items, 80 percent of the increase) but diffuse by risk, and the one binary item (the mass-impregnated order) is now more likely to resolve by signature than the first set allowed (no preferred-bidder position at the majors has fallen away in five years; conversion averages 9.6 months, so late April 2027 at the latest), with timing in H1 2027 the residual risk. The cone over 18 months is about ±6 percent at EBITDA, ±10 percent at EPS and ±30 percent at free cash flow, roughly symmetric around the second set's base, negative against consensus on all three lines and positive against a share price at the 5th percentile of its five-year multiple range. Disclosure is the weakest element: nothing on profit, margin or cash until 24 February 2027, so the next four months must be monitored through peers (Prysmian 29 October, NKT 19 November), customers, channels and costs, and the February results will carry six new facts at once. The trace from company statements to segment EBITDA is clear; below EBITDA it is not, and the company's silence on depreciation, tax and cash definitions is the main reason the brokers' EPS range is four times wider than their EBITDA range. The share price now moves 57 percent with the large-capitalisation electrification complex, so the path of the multiple over 18 months is industry-driven while its level (a 30 percent discount) is company-driven and resolvable. For the memo: carry the second set's base case with the three input changes; weight the February and July 2027 prints heavily; present EPS and cash with their bridges and bases shown; carry a downside in which the order slips two quarters and Connect's margin falls 50 basis points (FY2027 EBITDA about €850m, EPS about €7.10) and an upside in which Grid holds double-digit growth and Transmission reaches 16.3 percent (about €955m, €8.75); and treat the sector regime as a separate risk from delivery.

C-79

How narrow is the business perimeter (number of countries and business lines), how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

C4813th of 35

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement. The perimeter is narrow in product and wide in geography, and both are changing: the product range has narrowed to electrification cable, while the geographic spread has widened into the United States and Morocco. Predictability is moderate: three engines with different cycles make group EBITDA steadier than any one engine, but the model needs many moving parts (segments, regions, currencies, metal, scope changes, discontinued operations), and the key data arrive only half-yearly.

  • Product: three Electrification segments (Transmission 24 percent, Grid 22 percent, Connect 42 percent of H1 2026 standard sales) plus a shrinking metallurgy line; Industry and Solutions has gone.
  • Geography: 39 to 41 countries; 47 percent of standard sales in Europe, 19 percent in North America (about €1bn of current sales after Republic Wire), 12 percent in Asia-Pacific; Norway is 26.6 percent of standard sales (from 13.3 percent in 2023) because of Halden; Canada 11.6 percent; core Eurozone 22.4 percent.
  • Currencies: euro, krone, Canadian and US dollars, Turkish lira and others; translation unhedged by policy; about half the €13.3bn of foreign-exchange derivative notional matures within a year; only blended sensitivities are disclosed.
  • Disclosure cadence: quarterly sales and organic growth by segment; half-yearly segment EBITDA, backlog and cash; the nine-month update gave no profit data.
  • For predictability: the diversity of engines is a stabiliser (Grid offset Transmission in 2026), but the number of bases and restatements raises forecast error, especially below EBITDA.
D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

D6423rd of 35

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. About three-fifths of the expected change rests on strengths Nexans has demonstrated, in the current team's own year as well as in the previous team's record, and that three-fifths covers almost the whole of FY2026 and most of FY2027; the two-fifths that rests on strengths still to be shown is concentrated in FY2028 and in the lines (Connect's margin, Republic Wire's synergies, the mass-impregnated line after mid-2028, cash conversion) where consensus is most demanding and where the second set is already below consensus. The plausibility of the base case is therefore high for the next 18 months and moderate beyond, and the structure of the thesis is favourable in one important respect: the demonstrated strengths are the ones that produce the first proofs (a Transmission margin print at or above 15 percent in February and July 2027, Grid volume on new capacity), while the undemonstrated strengths are the ones the base case does not need until 2028. The thesis does not rely on Nexans acquiring a strength it has never shown in order to deliver FY2027; it does rely on two such strengths (competing in US low voltage; holding Connect's European margin without a construction recovery) to deliver the consensus FY2028 path, which is one reason I prefer the second set's FY2028 to consensus.

The bridge, classified by what it rests on (FY2025 €728m to FY2028 €1,013m, the second set's +€285m).

Driver Amount Rests on Evidence of the strength My confidence
Transmission contract vintage and Electra replacing charters about +€60m to €75m demonstrated margin +195 basis points on flat sales in H1 2026; Electra delivered on time and on budget (May 2026) and booked to end-2028; NKT (15.2 to 20.2 percent in a year) and Prysmian (to 21.2 percent) precedents; Deutsche Bank: 2028 margin supported by existing backlog high

Other Activities +€5m demonstrated metallurgy beat in Q3 high

The demonstrated and partly demonstrated rows sum to about €190m to €210m of the €285m, or roughly two-thirds; the rows that need a new strength sum to about €60m to €80m, and they fall almost entirely in FY2028. Over the next 18 months (FY2026 and FY2027), the share resting on demonstrated strengths is nearer 80 percent, because the FY2027 increase of €181m over FY2025 is Transmission vintage (€58m), Republic Wire scope (€72m), Grid volume (€37m) and small items.

The record, and whose record it is. The business model's record is good: initial guidance met or beaten in every year since 2022; every 2021 capital-markets-day target delivered; a margin doubled under SHIFT; Electra, the Halden doubling and the Charleroi tower delivered on time. T-D1 is right that the new team has already shown the strengths that matter for the next 18 months (Electra handover, 525 kV into production for LanWin2 in October 2026, La Triveneta synergies, a 7 percent H1 beat with a raise, Grid at 14.8 percent), and the first set is right that this is one year of evidence and that the people who built the record have left. The record also has a specific weakness that bears on exactly the undemonstrated rows: Nexans's history of trouble is a history of under-utilised high-voltage capacity (2012 to 2013, 2018), and the one line the thesis cannot yet prove is the loading of its most specialised capacity after mid-2028. The market's discount (about 30 percent on EV/EBITDA to Prysmian and NKT, Jefferies) is the price of that memory.

Strengths the business would need to acquire, and whether the base case needs them.

  • Competing in US building wire against an integrated duopoly at 4 percent share. Not needed for the base case through FY2028, which holds Republic Wire's margin flat and takes only the acquisition-case EBITDA plus modest synergies; needed for the consensus Connect path (€447m in FY2028) and for the upside case.
  • Restoring Connect's European margin without a construction recovery. Needed in part: the second set's Connect margin of 12.6 percent in FY2028 (from 11.5 percent) assumes input-cost recovery and synergies; about €15m to €20m of it is a new strength. Consensus's 13.0 percent needs more.
  • Running a 40 percent Grid capacity ramp at a stable margin. Partly a new strength; the second set already allows for start-up costs, which is the right treatment.
  • Loading the mass-impregnated line after mid-2028. A new strength in the sense that the pipeline is not visible (Deutsche Bank: relies on early-stage projects such as GREGY); the second set does not assume it (Transmission flat after FY2028), which is why the second set's FY2029 to FY2030 is the most defensible part of its forecast against consensus.
  • Converting above 45 percent of EBITDA to cash on a full basis. A new strength: not shown since 2023 on the company's definition, and not in the base case.
  • A new team through a downturn. Untested; the base case does not include a downturn.

Why the plausibility is high for 18 months and only moderate beyond. The FY2026 outcome is about 85 percent known (H1 reported, nine-month sales reported, guidance confirmed). FY2027's increase is contracted or acquired to about four-fifths. FY2028 asks for three things to arrive together (Halden loaded through mid-2028 and then something else, Grid capacity on time and at margin, a Connect margin recovery) in the same year as the 2028 target is judged. T-D1's "high for 2027, medium for 2028" is the right summary, and it argues for the memo to weight the next two prints (February and July 2027) heavily and the 2028 numbers lightly.

D-03In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?D5019th of 35full answer under 2a Internal risks
D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

B568th of 35

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The cone is narrow at EBITDA, moderate at earnings per share and wide at free cash flow, and its skew differs by the reference point: around the second set's base case it is roughly symmetric at EBITDA and slightly negative at EPS and cash; against consensus it is negative on all three lines; against the share price it is positive, because the multiple already sits at the 5th percentile of its five-year range and discounts a worse outcome than the cone's centre. For FY2027 adjusted EBITDA I put 80 percent of the probability between €860m and €960m around €909m (about ±6 percent), with 15 percent below €860m and 5 percent above €960m; the expected value is about €900m, a shade below the base. For FY2027 adjusted EPS the 80 percent band is about €7.10 to €8.75 around €8.04 (±10 percent), because the below-EBITDA items add their own uncertainty (depreciation €15m either way, tax 29 to 32 percent, the hedging line) on a smaller base. For FY2027 free cash flow the band is about €180m to €330m around €258m (±30 percent), because working capital on a flat backlog is the least predictable line and a single large award or its absence moves it by €50m to €100m. The case is therefore predictable in the sense that matters most for the next 18 months (the EBITDA path, where FY2026 is 85 percent known and FY2027 is four-fifths contracted) and unpredictable in the two lines on which consensus is most exposed (EPS and cash), which is why the predictability of the forecasts and the predictability of the consensus revisions are different questions with different answers.

The cone, line by line (FY2027, the last full year inside the 18 months).

Line Downside (about 15 percent) Base (about 65 percent) Upside (about 20 percent) What sets the edges
Adjusted EBITDA €850m to €860m €909m €940m to €960m replacement order slipping two quarters (-€25m); Connect margin ±50 basis points (±€16m); input costs ±€15m; Grid growth ±3 points (±€12m); Republic Wire margin ±€10m; an execution incident (tail)
Transmission margin 14.5 percent 15.6 percent 16.3 percent Halden loading; Electra utilisation; the mix of 525 kV, Mallorca-Menorca engineering and repair work
Adjusted EPS (45.0m diluted) €7.10 €8.04 €8.75 the EBITDA band, plus depreciation €290m to €320m, tax 29 to 32 percent, hedging gains or losses in net financial items
Free cash flow, full basis €180m €258m €330m working capital: a stall in awards (down-payments consumed, none received: -€75m) against a large award or a Great Sea Interconnector restart (+€75m); copper (a fall releases cash)
Net debt at end-2027 (company basis) about €800m about €690m about €600m the cash band, plus any acquisition (each €500m adds 0.5x)

The 18-month path matters as much as the end point. H2 2026 (reported 24 February 2027) is tightly bounded: the second set's €417m of EBITDA sits in a band of about €400m to €430m because H1 is known, guidance is confirmed and management says it is working to beat the midpoint. H1 2027 (reported late July 2027) is where the cone is widest in relative terms: it carries the replacement order's timing, the first full half of the 2027 energy step, the Grid capacity start-up costs and five months of Republic Wire scope at a time when US tariffs may or may not have been imposed; I put H1 2027 EBITDA between €410m and €460m around the second set's €439m.

The skew, by reference point.

  • Around the base case: EBITDA roughly symmetric, because the two largest swing items pull in opposite directions (Grid has beaten every expectation for two quarters; Transmission has missed on volume for two quarters) and because the second set's base already sits below consensus on the lines that consensus gets wrong. EPS slightly negative, for the depreciation reason in reading note C.7. Cash slightly negative, because the working-capital balancing loop (P-05) is in its early phase and the contract-liability balance of €1,137m at end-2025 has only one way to go while the backlog is flat.
  • Against consensus (EBITDA €936m, EPS €9.13, free cash flow €439m): clearly negative on all three; the probability that FY2027 EBITDA reaches €936m is about 25 percent, that adjusted net income reaches consensus's €376m about 30 percent, that free cash flow reaches €439m on any sensible definition below 15 percent.
  • Against the share price: positive. At €113.60 the shares trade at 10.4x consensus two-year-forward EPS and about 11.8x our corrected model EPS; the first set's trough arithmetic (about €102 on unchanged earnings at the ten-year 10th percentile; about €92 on a 10 percent earnings cut) against about €140 on a return to the ten-year median multiple on FY2027 delivery gives roughly 2.5 to 1 upside to downside around the base case. T-D2's "wide and positively skewed" describes this reference point, not the earnings cone.

Why the EBITDA cone is narrow, in evidence rather than assertion. Four-fifths of FY2027 EBITDA is contracted or already owned: Transmission's revenue is about 90 percent in backlog with fixed prices and metal indexation; two-thirds of Grid sits under multi-year frameworks; Republic Wire's scope is a consolidation fact; the legacy Transmission contracts that depressed 2025 margins finish by late 2026 (Bernstein) whatever happens to new orders. The historical dispersion of Nexans's own outcomes against initial guidance since 2022 is small and one-sided (top of range, middle, a 15 percent beat, in line, a raise), and the one large beat (FY2024) came from volume at Halden. The dispersion that T-D2 documents (2013, 2018) came from under-utilised high-voltage capacity without a backlog, and that condition does not hold through mid-2028.

Why the EPS and cash cones are wider, and why that is the part the market will trade. The broker range for FY2027 EPS (€7.72 to €11.78) is four times wider than the EBITDA range in percentage terms, and OWN-2 shows why: brokers cannot agree on depreciation (Deutsche Bank €327m in total, Hana €247m), on the tax rate (25.8 percent statutory, 32.7 percent reported) or on the share count (43.7m against 44.9m), and the Republic Wire purchase-price allocation is still provisional. The company guides neither EPS nor cash below the free-cash-flow range, and its cash target is on a basis most brokers do not model. So the lines that drive revisions and the fourth set's answers are the ones with the widest cones, and the February 2027 accounts (final purchase-price allocation, full-year depreciation, tax rate, working capital) are the event that narrows them.

The tails. On the downside, the correlated cluster the first set described (an idle Halden line weakening bargaining power on replacement work; a US price war coinciding with a tariff delay; an execution incident on a 525 kV project in its first industrial production) would take FY2027 EBITDA towards €800m and EPS towards €6; probability perhaps 5 to 8 percent inside 18 months. On the upside, a Great Sea Interconnector restart with manufacturing from 2028 (the chief executive's stated target) adds nothing to FY2027 EBITDA but would add down-payments to 2027 cash and remove the post-mid-2028 question; probability of a binding restart decision inside 18 months perhaps 30 percent.

So how predictable is the case? More predictable than its share price behaviour and its sector reputation suggest, and less predictable than its EBITDA cone alone would imply, because the lines the market prices (EPS, cash) carry definitional and below-the-line uncertainty that the company does little to resolve. For the next 18 months I would characterise it as: EBITDA predictable to within about 6 percent; EPS to within about 10 percent; cash to within about 30 percent; share price driven 57 percent by the sector (D-07) and, for the rest, by three dated events.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

F2834th of 35

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The disclosure is helpful on volume and unhelpful on everything the thesis actually turns on, and the gap is widest in exactly the window asked about: between now and 24 February 2027 the company will publish nothing on profit, margin, cash or working capital, having withheld all of them at the nine-month update, and the two drivers that decide FY2027 (the H2 2026 Transmission margin and the replacement order) can only be inferred, the first from nothing and the second from a press release that may or may not come. What the company does disclose (quarterly standard sales and organic growth by segment, the backlog each quarter, dated capacity milestones, acquisition terms, the guidance assumptions) is specific and reliable, and it lets the Consumer track Grid and Connect momentum well. The practical consequence is that monitoring over the next six months must lean on external and peer data more than on Nexans, and that the February results will carry an unusual amount of new information at once (second-half margins by segment, free cash flow and working capital, the Republic Wire purchase-price allocation and first synergy figure, the depreciation run-rate, the tax rate, the FY2027 guide, and possibly the order), which is itself a risk: a day on which six things are learned is a day on which the market can misread any one of them.

What is disclosed, how often, and how useful it is for each driver.

Driver of the thesis What Nexans discloses Next disclosure Usefulness over six months

What has got worse, and what it signals. The nine-month release of 22 October 2026 was the first in recent years to omit profit and cash data, and the company has not explained the change. The first set read it as a regression from a well-regarded disclosure standard; I read it also as a tell: a company confident of its H2 margin and of the order's signature would have had every reason to say so on a day the shares fell 7 percent. The absence of comment is not evidence that either has gone wrong, but it removes the ability to distinguish "fine and quiet" from "slipping and quiet" until February, which is precisely the ambiguity a monitoring framework exists to avoid. The disclosure of the Mallorca-Menorca preferred-supplier status on the same day, with its six-month engineering phase and mid-2027 EPCI signature, was helpful but also illustrates the company's habit of announcing positions before contracts, which the first set noted and which OWN-1 shows has a 9.6-month average lag at peers.

What the Consumer can monitor instead over the six months.

  • Peers: Prysmian's Q3 (29 October 2026) and NKT's Q3 (19 November 2026) for Transmission margins, award pricing, down-payment terms and any comment on an awards "air pocket"; Siemens Energy's targets (11 November) for grid pricing; both peers' full-year results in February 2027. NKT's and Prysmian's Transmission margins are the best available proxy for the vintage effect Nexans is executing, since all three execute the same 2023 to 2026 price regime.
  • Customers and tenders: Red Eléctrica's Mallorca-Menorca filings; IPTO and Greek-Cypriot government statements on the Great Sea Interconnector (seabed surveys were due to resume from 26 October 2026); TenneT call-offs under the 525 kV framework; Enedis and Nordic utility tenders; any Mediterranean interconnector award notice (Terna, ONEE, Egyptian authorities).
  • Channels and prices: Rexel's Q4 (February) for European and US low-voltage volume and price; US building-wire price announcements by Southwire and Prysmian; the COMEX-LME copper spread and the tariff proclamation due before January 2027; Nordic housing permits.
  • Costs: TTF gas and European power forwards for 2027 (the hedge roll-off); European polyethylene contract prices; Hormuz transit data (Suez transits recovered to 66 percent of 2023 levels, Morgan Stanley, 21 October 2026).
  • Capital allocation: the weekly buyback filings (size tells whether the programme is still anti-dilutive only); any acquisition announcement and its multiple.
  • The register: threshold filings (Baillie Gifford, BlackRock, Bpifrance) and any activist appearance after Quiñenco's exit.

Score reasoning. The structure of disclosure (segment margins, backlog, guidance assumptions, capacity dates, synergy splits) is above the sector average and the first set's C-72 was right to call the guidance specific; the frequency and the October omission make it below average for the window in question. The two combine to a modest negative for predictability over six months and a neutral one over twelve.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

C5010th of 35

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The trace is clear from the company's statements to the operating drivers and to the segment EBITDA lines, and it breaks down between EBITDA and earnings per share, where the company gives no guidance and where the items that matter (depreciation, net financial items, tax, the share count) are either undisclosed in schedule form or provisional. The external transmission is clear and well lagged for the two drivers that carry the thesis (high-voltage contract prices into executed Transmission margin with a two-to-three-year lag; utility capital spending into Grid volume, capacity permitting) and clear but confusing for metal (copper reaches revenue, working capital and reported profit but not adjusted EBITDA), and it is opaque for the non-metal costs that bear on Connect and Grid in 2027, where the lag is set by an undisclosed hedging book and by framework renegotiation. The practical result is that the Consumer can build the memo's fundamental milestones with confidence down to segment EBITDA, must build the EPS bridge from the accounts and the brokers' bridge (OWN-2) rather than from anything the company says, and should expect the market's own trace to be noisier than ours because half of the brokers do not do this work.

The trace from statements to lines (the milestones and measures the memo should carry).

Where the trace breaks: EBITDA to earnings per share. The company guides neither EPS nor any item between EBITDA and net income. The four items that decide the conversion are each uncertain in a way the company could remove and has not: (1) depreciation, where H1 2026's €153.4m is the only hard datum, the Electra vessel's useful life is undisclosed (20 to 30 years is the plausible range, worth about €6m a year of difference) and the Republic Wire purchase-price allocation is provisional (goodwill €474m; the split between goodwill and 14-year customer relationships decides €5m to €15m a year of amortisation that adjusted earnings exclude and reported earnings do not); (2) net financial items, where the debt is fixed-rate and simple (€400m at 5.5 percent to April 2028, €575m at 4.125 percent to 2029, €350m at 4.25 percent to 2030, €500m at 4.25 percent to 2031, plus a €200m EIB loan at 1.93 percent maturing in April 2027) but the H1 2026 line carried a €35m "other financial" charge from hedging that may or may not recur; (3) tax, where the reported rate (30.9 percent in 2025, 32.7 percent in H1 2026) sits well above the 25.8 percent theoretical rate because of unrecognised deferred tax assets and country mix, and where Deutsche Bank's expectation of tax benefits from the Republic Wire asset-purchase structure is the one potential positive the company has not quantified; (4) the share count, where 1.29m free and performance shares (2.95 percent) are offset by anti-dilutive buybacks, so the diluted count is stable at about 45.0m but brokers use 43.7m to 44.9m. Together these produce a FY2027 EPS range across brokers of €7.72 to €11.78 on an EBITDA range of €898m to €1,012m, which is the clearest measure of how unclear the trace is below EBITDA. The second set's bridge (depreciation €300m, net financial items -€85m, tax 30 percent, minorities €5m, 45.0m shares) is the one the memo should carry, and the Consumer should present EPS with that bridge shown.

The external transmission, dynamic by dynamic.

  • High-voltage award prices to Transmission margin: clear and lagged. Prices per kilometre doubled between the 2019 to 2020 and 2024 to 2026 award vintages; the margin arrives as contracts execute, two to three years later; the first set and T-D1 show the same lag at NKT and Prysmian. The transmission is one-directional through 2028 (prices in backlog are fixed and metal-indexed) and reverses only for awards made from about 2028 for post-2030 delivery.
  • Utility capital spending to Grid volume: clear and capacity-limited. European utility network investment runs 10 to 12 percent above last year; the EU Grids Package and Enedis's framework are law-dated; Nexans converts as fast as its capacity lands. The lag is the commissioning schedule, which the company dates.
  • Data-centre spending to Connect North America and Grid: clear but concentrated and financially fragile; Rexel's US same-day sales (+18.2 percent in Q3 2026) are the best monthly proxy; a financing-led pause among neoclouds (Deutsche Bank, 27 October 2026) is the risk the company cannot see earlier than the market.
  • European construction to Connect Europe: clear, slow and negative; 53 months of contraction; no recovery before 2027 to 2028; Nordic housing permits are the lead indicator.
  • Copper to the accounts: clear once understood, confusing in the headlines. A 10 percent copper move changes current-metal revenue by 4 to 5 percent, working capital in the same direction, reported (not adjusted) operating income through the Core exposure revaluation (+€75.2m in H1 2026 on 38,665 tonnes of unhedged core inventory), and the after-tax hit of a 10 percent rise is €26m (2025 accounts) on working capital and financing. Adjusted EBITDA and adjusted EPS are not affected; reported EPS is, in either direction.
  • Energy and polymers to Connect and Grid margins: opaque. Energy is bought at least 18 months ahead under an Energy Committee with undisclosed volumes and strikes; the September 2026 price spike therefore reaches costs mainly from mid-2027; polymer compound comes from a single supplier under undisclosed indexation; recovery through price is daily in Connect for metals but lagged by framework renegotiation for everything else. The 2010 precedent (70 percent recovered, €70m cost) and the 2022 precedent (€60m a year for European cable makers) bound the exposure but the company gives no sensitivity.
  • Currency: the krone (26.6 percent of standard sales) transmits with the opposite sign to intuition (a 10 percent fall adds €28m to €32m of net income and takes about €140m off equity); the US dollar the usual way (a 10 percent fall costs €21m). Only blended sensitivities are disclosed.
  • Rates and the French sovereign: reach the multiple, not the earnings; the debt is fixed and the next refinancing is April 2027 (€200m EIB at 1.93 percent), April 2028 (€400m at 5.5 percent, to be repaid from cash on the second set's path).

Overall. Down to segment EBITDA the case traces as well as any in the sector and better than most; the company's own bridge by segment and its dated capacity plan are the backbone of the memo's milestones. Below EBITDA and in cash it traces poorly, and the company's silence on depreciation, tax and the cash-target definition is the main reason the market's EPS and cash expectations are both dispersed and, on our numbers, too high.

What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

A503rd of 35

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement. Sentiment risks are significant but partly discounted: the shares have already fallen 24 percent in six months, sit at the 5th percentile of their five-year forward multiple and at the 18th percentile of relative strength. The remaining sudden risks come from the cable group and the artificial-intelligence and rates complex, and they arrive in the next three months.

  • Peer read-across. Prysmian's Q3 2026 results (29 October 2026) and NKT's (19 November 2026): any margin disappointment or comment on pricing, down-payment terms or an award "air pocket" would hit the group; NKT's Karlskrona ramp (Q2 2027) is a sector-wide execution test. The peer group explains a large share of daily moves in each cable stock.
  • Capacity-overhang narrative. Morgan Stanley's supply-demand model (parity about 2030; about 15 percent oversupply if European offshore wind runs at 13 GW a year), Chinese suppliers targeting European tenders, LS Cable's TenneT awards: each new datapoint can de-rate the group years before prices fall, which is how the sector has traded historically.
  • Artificial-intelligence financing. The theme is cracking at its financing (credit spreads of neoclouds and hyperscalers wider; Goldman Sachs's risk appetite indicator negative for the first time since March 2026); a data-centre capital-expenditure pause would hit Nexans's Connect North America and Grid narrative.
  • Rates and Europe. A US ten-year yield near 5.1 percent with a December Federal Reserve hike priced; Brent at about $111; French sovereign spreads at decade highs spreading to Italy; the house's Momentum tilt moved to Underweight. A French political risk premium into the 2027 presidential election attaches to a French-listed name regardless of its revenue mix.
  • US offshore wind and energy policy shocks (cancellations, Inflation Reduction Act changes) touch Charleston's subsea order book.

C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

C5813th of 35

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement. The company is an "honest tracker" with a conservative bias: it guides prudently ("a question of prudence"), raises at the half-year when the first half allows, confirms at the nine months, and rarely makes large organic upgrades. The new chief executive has followed the same script in his first year, with the one upgrade driven by an acquisition.

  • Record: FY2022 guidance €580m to €600m, actual €599m; FY2023 €640m to €700m, actual €665m; FY2024 €670m to €730m, actual €804m (as first reported), a material beat after a mid-year raise; FY2025 raised mid-year, landing about €7m below the revised midpoint; FY2026 initial €730m to €810m called "surprisingly light", raised to €770m to €840m in July (mostly Republic Wire), confirmed in October despite the sales beat.
  • Mid-term: every 2021 capital-markets-day indicator was met; the 2028 targets set in November 2024 have been reaffirmed but never raised, unlike peers who set new ambitions above consensus (NKT in November 2025; Prysmian raising its 2028 targets).
  • Hueber's behaviour: "I want to be very clear. We will continue to execute the road map"; conservative on politically sensitive items (the Great Sea Interconnector excluded); confident in tone on Transmission margins.
C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

C6014th of 35

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement. Over one, three and five years the record is one of meeting or beating, with modest margins of beat and one material beat (FY2024); there has been no profit warning since 2018. In comparable conditions in the past (project execution stress combined with weak construction, as in 2013 and 2018), the company missed badly, which is why the market discounts the current record more than a peer's.

  • Last year: H1 2026 adjusted EBITDA €387.7m against compiled consensus €364m (+7 percent), Transmission EBITDA €106.5m against €91m (+17 percent), free cash flow €165.5m against €70m; Connect margin 11.8 percent against 12.2 percent (miss), Grid 15.4 percent against 15.8 percent (miss); Q3 2026 standard sales €1,698m against €1,633m to €1,643m (+3.4 to +4 percent), organic +9.0 percent against 4.6 to 5.1 percent, Transmission organic -4.0 percent against about -1.4 to -1.7 percent (miss); no Q3 profit disclosed. Q4 2025 organic growth 11.8 percent (beat); Q3 2025 Connect organic growth missed.
  • Three years: FY2023 €665m against €640m to €700m (in line); FY2024 €804m (as first reported) against €670m to €730m (+15 percent, beat); FY2025 about €7m below the revised midpoint (in line); H1 2025 €441m against €423m (+4.2 percent).
  • Five years: FY2022 €599m against €580m to €600m (beat at the midpoint); every 2021 capital-markets-day indicator met.
  • Comparable conditions: two profit warnings in 2013 and a crisis in 2018 (high-voltage execution problems); three guidance revisions in eighteen months then; sell-side bears were right in the short term in about 80 percent of past execution stumbles. Current conditions share one feature with those episodes (under-utilised high-voltage capacity at Halden) but not the others (the company now has a contracted backlog, down-payments, a simpler portfolio and a conservative guide).
P-06

After assessing the track record of delivery in greater detail, how much weight should the forecasts for the next 18 months to four years carry, and in which direction should they be leaned?

C-5215th of 35

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. The forecasts deserve high weight for FY2026, moderate weight for FY2027, low-to-moderate weight for FY2028 and low weight for FY2029 and FY2030. FY2026 is about 85 percent known: H1 adjusted EBITDA of €387.7m is reported, nine-month sales are reported, guidance was confirmed on 22 October and the company has landed inside or above its range for five years. FY2027 rests on contracted volume (about 90 percent of Transmission revenue in backlog; two-thirds of Grid under frameworks) but also on one unsigned order (the replacement for Halden's mass-impregnated line), unindexed input costs and a leadership team that has never run the company through a downturn. FY2028 adds dependence on mid-2028 Halden loading, Grid capacity arriving on time and Republic Wire's synergies; FY2029 and FY2030 depend on post-scarcity award prices and on how the balance sheet is deployed. The lean differs by line, and this is the most useful point for the next set: for EBITDA, my base is neutral against consensus for FY2026 and 3 to 4 percent below it for FY2027 and FY2028, with my own skew around that base roughly symmetric (Grid upside offsets Transmission timing risk); for EPS and free cash flow, my base is clearly below consensus (EPS -9 to -12 percent on the brokers' basis; FCF well below), and that lean is high-conviction because it rests on arithmetic (D&A, tax rate, leases, the earnings basis) rather than on operating judgement. Relative to my own base, lean upward on Grid revenue and on capital deployment (acquisitions or buybacks add 4 to 10 percent to FY2028 EPS), and downward on Transmission volume timing in FY2027 and on the Connect margin.

The delivery record, and whose record it is.

  • The pattern is an "honest tracker" with a conservative bias: guide prudently, raise at the half-year if the first half allows, confirm at nine months. Misses in the company's history came in clusters tied to high-voltage execution (two profit warnings in 2013; the 2018 crisis, when sell-side bears were right in the short term about 80 percent of the time and drawdowns reached 30 to 50 percent).
  • The 2018 to 2025 record belongs to a chief executive and finance director who left in 2025; I treat it as evidence about the business model and its controls. The new team's record in 2026: H1 EBITDA beat consensus by 7 percent and free cash flow by €95m; Q3 Grid and Connect organic growth beat; but Transmission organic growth missed (-4.0 percent against about -1.7 percent), Connect and Grid H1 margins missed by 40 basis points each, the replacement order slipped from "within weeks" (early October) and the nine-month release withheld profit and cash data.

How much weight, quantified as a cone (adjusted EBITDA).

My base Plausible range Width What sets the edges
FY2026 €805m €790m to €825m about ±2% Q4 Transmission comparison (+40% a year earlier) against Grid momentum
FY2027 €909m €850m to €960m about ±6% replacement order timing (about €25m for two quarters of slip); Connect margin ±50 bp (about €16m); input costs (about €15m); Grid growth ±3 points (about €12m)
FY2028 €1,013m €920m to €1,100m about ±9% Halden after mid-2028; 2027 award prices; Grid capacity timing; Republic Wire synergies

That distribution sits mostly below the FactSet median of €936m, and its centre sits about where the lower half of the broker range does (Deutsche Bank €898m, AlphaValue €902m, CIC €899m, Oddo BHF €922m).

The direction of the lean, line by line.

  • Revenue (standard sales): neutral to slightly positive against consensus brokers on the standard basis (my FY2027 €7,184m against €7,084m to €7,460m), because Grid is stronger than they model and Transmission weaker.
  • EBITDA: neutral for FY2026; down 3 to 4 percent for FY2027 and FY2028 against consensus. The difference is Transmission volume (consensus segment revenue €1,864m and €2,053m against my €1,680m and €1,798m) and the Connect margin (consensus 12.6 and 13.0 percent against my 11.8 and 12.6 percent), partly offset by Grid (consensus €248m and €263m against my €255m and €282m).
  • EPS: down. My adjusted EPS (€6.38, €8.04, €9.56) is 9 to 12 percent below the brokers' basis (€7.03, €9.13, €10.88). About 9 points of that is basis; the rest is my 30 percent tax rate against an implied 27 to 29 percent, and the FY2028 EBITDA difference.
  • FCF: down, with high conviction: about €240m to €260m in FY2026 and FY2027 and €360m in FY2028 on a full basis. Consensus figures (€309m, €439m, €534m) are each broker's own definition, mostly before lease payments and reorganisation cash.
  • Dividend: neutral (€3.10, €3.40, €3.75 against €3.12, €3.40, €3.79); the policy is progressive and the payout floor (30 percent) is far below the actual payout.

What would move the weight. Higher weight for FY2027 to FY2028: signature of the replacement order with its value and loading profile; the FY2026 results (24 February 2027) disclosing a second-half Transmission margin of about 15 percent and a cash figure inside the guided range; an organic FY2027 guide. Lower weight: no signed order by the end of the first quarter of 2027; a FY2027 guide whose midpoint sits below €880m; a Connect margin below 11 percent in H2 2026.
