Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Nexans (NEX-FR), a French-listed cable maker that completed its exit from non-electrification businesses in July 2026; it reports PWR-Transmission (high-voltage subsea and land cable systems with installation: the Halden plant in Norway with its mass-impregnated and extruded lines, Charleston in the United States, Charleroi in Belgium and three cable-laying vessels including Electra), PWR-Grid (medium-voltage cables and accessories for distribution utilities), PWR-Connect (low-voltage cables for buildings, industry and data centres, including Republic Wire in the United States since June 2026) and Other Activities (metallurgy and copper rod).

Request: OWN-1

Question: Nexans (NEX-FR) announced in July 2026 that it was preferred bidder for a replacement mass-impregnated subsea cable order intended to load the mass-impregnated line at its Halden plant from the end of 2026 to mid-2028 after the Great Sea Interconnector was postponed, and the Mallorca-Menorca 3 preferred-supplier award it announced with Red Eléctrica on 22 October 2026 uses extruded XLPE alternating-current cable (Spanish regulatory filing of 22 October 2026, reported by Bernstein Research the same day), so it is not that order. Which project is the July 2026 mass-impregnated order (customer, route, voltage, length, value, delivery schedule and technology), what have the customer, regulators, governments and the press in Italy, Greece, Egypt, Morocco, Tunisia, Cyprus and Spain said about it since July 2026, has the EPCI contract been signed and on what terms (down-payments, reservation fees, liquidated damages), and which further deep-water or mass-impregnated interconnector projects (for example the Greece-Egypt GREGY link, Terna's Tyrrhenian and Sardinia-Corsica-Italy links, the Italy-Tunisia ELMED link, Morocco links and the Great Sea Interconnector, whose cable production Nexans's chief executive said in October 2026 is targeted to restart in 2028) could load the Halden mass-impregnated line after mid-2028, with each project's status, tender timetable, competing bidders and the technology chosen?

Request: OWN-2

Question: What did Prysmian's Q3 2026 results on 29 October 2026, Siemens Energy's capital markets day on 11 November 2026, NKT's Q3 2026 results on 19 November 2026, and the latest statements of Rexel, Southwire, Atkore and Cenergy Holdings say about high-voltage transmission margins and backlog execution, new award pricing and down-payment or reservation-fee terms, transmission system operator re-phasing of projects, European low-voltage demand and pricing, US building-wire prices, capacity and the scheduled Section 232 refined-copper tariffs, and non-metal input costs (polymers, energy, freight) after the 2026 Strait of Hormuz disruption; how did each company's shares react and how did brokers change their estimates; and what read-across do brokers and investors draw for Nexans (NEX-FR), whose FY2026 results and first FY2027 guidance are due on 24 February 2027 and whose FY2026 guidance (adjusted EBITDA of €770m to €840m, free cash flow of €235m to €325m, confirmed 22 October 2026) was last raised on 29 July 2026?

Request: OWN-3

Question: How is Nexans (NEX-FR) discussed by investors since July 2026, as reported in broker feedback from investor calls, roadshows and conferences (for example Deutsche Bank's "Feedback from investor call with management" of 26 October 2026), in fund letters and fund-manager interviews, in short-interest and securities-lending data, in shareholder threshold filings since Quiñenco's sale of 5 percent on 24 February 2026, and on social media such as X (Twitter), Reddit and investor forums: what are the bull and bear arguments investors cite, which catalysts and risks they name (the replacement mass-impregnated order, Transmission margins, Republic Wire, free cash flow, capital allocation, the Great Sea Interconnector, French sovereign risk), how they explain the shares' discount to Prysmian and NKT (about 30 percent on EV/EBITDA according to Jefferies on 11 October 2026) and what valuation multiple brokers and investors argue the shares deserve and why, whether any activist, hedge fund or large holder is reported to be building a position or pressing for share buybacks or a change of strategy after the Quiñenco exit and the 34.84 percent vote against a director's renewal at the May 2025 annual meeting, and how investor sentiment towards the European cable group as a whole (Prysmian, NKT, Cenergy) is described?
